## Summary

The Failure & Trail System is the pure-reader renderer that turns the Life-Support Graph's failure descriptors into physical marks on the ship (mist, frost fan, scorch, puddle, hairline -> crack -> bent rib), in three sizes, thickest at the source, with density from flow and length from age; it holds no state, registers no block, hook or command, writes only canvas, material and particle output, and is the truth channel that no hallucination may add to, alter or remove. Read this GDD if you design, art-direct or build anything that shows a fault, tells a player where a fault is, lights a fault, or sits beside the trails (Emitter State, Lighting, the Perception Filter, Horror Entities, In-World Instruments, Onboarding, Derelict Authoring): it fixes the descriptor-to-visual table of record for all seventeen descriptor ids, the growth, reach, dullness and budget formulas with their worked values, the tell-window and faster-than-walking invariants, and 143 acceptance criteria. It does not simulate propagation or atmosphere, plays no sound, and has no UI of its own.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` (systems-index enumeration #9, Recommended Design Order 8; Category Gameplay) · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Life-Support Graph`, `Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera` (both designed); `Command & Simulation Layer` (designed, transitive); `Ship Section & Layout Data Model` (undesigned, hard: anchor geometry and room surfaces, provisional). Depended on by: Onboarding (index, undesigned) and Emitter State (EM-17, soft, designed); neighbours that must repeat or reject their rows when written: Lighting, Perception Filter, Horror Entities, In-World Instruments, Adaptive Audio, Interaction, Derelict Authoring, Settings, Co-op.

## Overview

The Failure & Trail System turns the Life-Support Graph's failure descriptors into physical marks on the ship: it is the **skin that shows the bruise**. It is a pure reader and a pure renderer. Every frame it reads the Graph's true failure state (`failures()` descriptors, the compartment bands and true temperature, `fuel_puddle_ml`) through the one read channel `read_view`, and draws, for each descriptor that has a consequence-channel medium, a mark in that loop's own vocabulary at the fitting, seam, opening frame or rib where the failure sits: **mist drifts** where air escapes, a **static crystalline frost fan** where coolant weeps, a **sharp radial scorch** where power arcs or a machine overheats, a **glossy, flat, ember-edged puddle** where fuel drips, a **hairline that becomes a crack and then a bent rib** where the hull gives. Every mark is thickest at its source, in one of three sizes (48, 96 and 144 base px) chosen by severity, with density chosen by flow and length chosen by age. The system holds no state: it registers no block, no step hook and no command, owns no propagation and no atmosphere (both are Graph state), writes only `CanvasItem`, `Material` and particle properties, and every visual property of every mark is a pure function of the Graph's current values and the render clock. That statelessness is what makes it a **truth channel**: a trail is on the Graph's closed consequence-channel list, so the Perception Filter may neither remove, alter nor add one, and a hallucination that lies about a machine's sprite or gauge cannot lie about the floor beside it.

The player never operates this system and never sees a menu for it. They meet it as the ship's handwriting. They wake in the cryo bay and their chest lamp catches a frost fan running from a lagged elbow toward the far wall; they follow it to its thickest point and find the fitting; they close the valve and watch the drip stop while the fan stays, still and dulling; they weld on a derelict section and watch mist spread along the seam bead; they hear a hiss behind a dark panel and walk to it because the mist will be there once the lamp arrives. Trails are the visible half of the Graph's two-channel warning: the fairness gate guarantees that a sev >= 1 descriptor exists for at least `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (10 s) before any endogenous loss, and this system is what makes that descriptor *seen*, with a growth rate bounded so a far-elbow trail crosses a six-tile room (288 base px) inside the 10-30 s tell window, and so a sev-4 trail on a single edge is strictly faster than walking and no faster than running. The same grammar, dulled and stilled, is the verdict of a dead derelict: a frost fan with no leak is coolant, a radial scorch at a junction box is power, an ember-edged stain is fuel, branching hairlines through ribs are the hull, and nothing at all where the air went.

The index describes the system as a pure reader that maps "graph failure state and footprint sources" to trail visuals. This GDD reconciles the second half: the Graph persists no footprint record and emits no footprint-tagged descriptor (the M17 `deposit_trace` kinds surface only as ordinary hp, temperature and section-rate changes), so this system draws **Graph descriptors only**; a hidden entity's web-grammar marks (smear, branching rime, dust direction, filament twitch) belong to Horror Entities and share only the floor-marks draw band. Without this system the Graph is invisible: Pillar 1's design test ("a hidden stat vs a visibly leaking pipe -> the pipe") has no pipe to draw, the tell Pillar 2 promises is a number nobody sees, and Pillar 4's guarantee that "no lie is unfalsifiable" has no physical witness to check a false gauge against. The MVP hypothesis (`design/gdd/game-concept.md`, MVP item 2: "a visible O2 / heat / power graph with propagating, physically rendered failures") cannot be tested without it. It is a Feature-layer system (it holds a render of Core state, not state) with two designed hard dependencies, the Life-Support Graph and the Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, and one hard dependency that is not yet designed, the Ship Section & Layout Data Model, whose anchor geometry and room surfaces this system reads through a provisional contract (Dependencies). The MVP slice (what ships first and what waits) is FT-38.

*Implementation questions raised while designing and deferred to ADRs (-> `/architecture-decision`): the split between the notification handler and the render callback (PR-8 rule 3); stepped sprite frames vs `GPUParticles2D` for moving media (spike-gated); the per-instance reveal-threshold and coverage-rank plumbing that keeps alpha binary; a pure `RefCounted` resolver `descriptor -> TrailSpec` so the decision logic is headless-testable; z-index numbering inside the floor-marks band; and the Perception Filter's truth-channel mask route (Filter-owned).*

## Player Fantasy

**Framing: direct, with a forensic depth and a witness guarantee.** The player knowingly reads failure as marks the ship leaves on itself and follows them to their source. *(creative-director consulted; Framing A "follow the mark to the wound" adopted, with B "the room remembers" as its depth in the past tense and C "the one thing you can still trust" as its guarantee. The Graph adopted the same layered shape; here the direct layer is the player's own act of reading.)*

> The ship cannot speak, but it cannot lie. When a loop fails, the failure writes itself onto the hull in the loop's own hue: soft drifting mist where air escapes, a static crystalline frost fan where coolant weeps, a sharp radial scorch where power arcs, a glossy ember-edged puddle under a fuel flange, a hairline that becomes a crack and then a bent rib where the hull gives. Every mark is thickest at its source. The player's chest lamp finds it; their eye follows it; their hands end at the fitting that failed.
>
> Competence is reading. A veteran crew is not faster with a wrench; it sees the frost before the gauge drops, knows a scorch from a stain, and knows an old isolated leak (still, dulled, no drip) from a live one. On a dead derelict the same marks are a verdict: frost fan, coolant; radial scorch, power; ember stain, fuel; branching hairlines, the hull; and nothing at all where the air went.
>
> And the floor never lies. When sanity slips, the gauge can read green, the scrubber can spin, the pipe's edge can bleed violet; the mist at its outlet does not change. No hallucination adds, alters or removes a mark. When the machine and the floor disagree, the floor wins.

**Emotion.** *Dread-literacy.* The player is a tracker reading a wounded body. The power is not control and not speed; it is **certainty earned by looking**: the click of following a mark back to the fitting that made it. Under threat that certainty is the only one the crew has left. The Graph's fantasy is caretaking under threat (the circulation); this system's is the skin that shows the bruise. In the Pillar 1 voice: *"Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth."* and *"Physical consequences. A pipe may look fixed, but the room still gets cold, the air still thins, the pressure still drops."* (partial quotation by design: the concept's human-channel bullet for Pillar 1 pre-dates the chest-mounted lamp decision and is not quoted here). Design test from the concept: *a hidden stat vs a visibly leaking pipe -> the pipe*; and from the visual anchor: *a "COOLANT LOW" banner vs a frosted elbow with a drip -> the elbow.*

**MDA and motivation.** Primary aesthetic: Challenge, delivered as competence by literacy (reading faster, not repairing faster). *(The concept ranks Discovery first and Challenge second at game level; this system ranks Challenge first locally because its job is the concept's Challenge row, a visible life-support graph that fails in traceable ways, while the verdict grammar, ET-4, delivers Discovery; this is not drift.)* Secondary: Discovery (the verdict grammar on derelicts: "what killed this ship?"). Tertiary: Fellowship (the floor is common ground two crewmates can both point at). Competence is served by legible cause, traceable origin and no hidden threshold; autonomy by a mark that tells the player what is *true* and never what to *do* (valve, patch, retreat or ignore stays theirs); relatedness by identical trails on every client, which lets crewmates verify each other (a VS-B property; see the Dependencies row for Co-op).

**Anchor moment: the first mark (cryo bay, minute one).** You wake. Your chest lamp (beam origin = the per-facing `chest_point`, hands free) falls across the deck and catches a fan of Coolant Ice frost running from a lagged elbow to the far wall. Nothing in the room moves faster than the drip at its root. Your eye does the work before you do: the fan is thickest at the elbow, so you walk to it. You close the upstream valve: the drip stops, but the fan stays, **still, not gone** (the Graph's isolated-leak descriptor has `intensity_pm` 0 while its mode stays, `LSG:127`). The heater warms the bay (8 mC per Graph step, about 2.5 minutes to COMFORT, `LSG:839`) and the fan's reach pulls back toward a local stamp at the elbow. The wall gauge finally agrees with the pipe. The tutorial is physical, and the Graph fixture is authored so the leak is already old when you arrive (a flagged requirement on the fixture; see Open Questions). Feel: quiet competence, "I read that."

**Experience targets (retained-capture scenes; every number is a locked value or a flagged placeholder).**

| # | Moment | What the player sees and feels | What guarantees it |
|---|---|---|---|
| ET-1 | The first mark (above) | One trail, thickest at its source, a valve that stops the drip but not the fan, the room slowly warming; quiet competence | One trail per source, no orphan trail (`LSG:970`); the T -2000 mC cryo-bay fixture (`LSG:839`); FT-3, FT-11, FT-17 |
| ET-2 | The sentence that grows (a weld seam weeps O2) | Soft drifting mist at the Hull Grey bead, spreading along the seam line while you watch; dread with time to act | The seam weeps from the step it exists (`LSG:228`); growth such that a 6-tile room (288 base px) is crossed in at most 28.8 s at the sev-1 floor and 14.4 s at the `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[1]` placeholder of 2 px/step; the seam-weep severity is **not stated by the Graph** (provisional, FT-14); FT-8, FT-9 |
| ET-3 | Retreat (breach rhythm) | A rib cracks; at sev 4 the mark jumps from hairline to bent rib and races along the rib faster than walking (`V_WALK` 2 px/tick = 12 px per Graph step) and no faster than running (`V_RUN` 3 px/tick = 18 px per Graph step): the `[PLACEHOLDER]` 15 px/step is 150 px/s. Walk and it beats you; run and you beat it. Behind it the cascade front lights the next edge one hop per Graph step. Flight | `ART:224` ("a failure trail propagating along a graph edge faster than you can walk"); FT-6, FT-9, FT-10 |
| ET-4 | The verdict (a dead derelict) | A greyed frost fan at a lagged elbow, a matte radial scorch at a junction box, an ember-edged stain at a flange, and **no mist anywhere** (O2 failure leaves no trail: read it from absence); you know what killed the ship before you touch anything. Forensic discovery | Dead faults are Graph faults at `hp` <= `HP_FAIL` on an unpowered branch with `intensity_pm` 0 and large `since_steps` (`LSG:226`), so the dried trail is a pure function and not a decal; FT-17, FT-18 |
| ET-5 | The floor disagrees (hallucination) | The scrubber renders whole, its rotor turning, its gauge green (a false state on the corruptible module-body channel); at its outlet fitting, soft mist still drifts, exactly as at full sanity. Vertigo, then grip: *the floor is still mine* | The closed consequence-channel list (`LSG:168`); FT-2, FT-24; the bit-identical capture (`PIPE:989`) |
| ET-6 | The stilled leak (end of a long session) | The corridor carries the ship's recent history: a coolant fan at an elbow whose valve you closed, dulling now; the seam bead still weeping where the weld has not yet worn in; the soot of a heat event. You can count the wounds the crew survived, as marks that are *true*, not as a score | Isolated leaks keep their mode with `intensity_pm` 0 (`LSG:127`); seam weeps persist until wear-in (`LSG:170`); FT-17. A patched pipe is **not** one of these: see the anti-promises |

*Session emotional arc carried by trails:* calm reading (ET-1) -> watchful tending (ET-2) -> forensic curiosity (ET-4) -> flight (ET-3) -> vertigo and grip (ET-5) -> ownership by history (ET-6). Flow entry is ET-1's single trail. The intentional flow breaks are the s -> m -> l jumps: a severity change is a discrete `F/` key event, so the jump is sudden by construction (FT-6).

**Design test.** *When a choice makes a failure easier to notice from a marker vs easier to follow from the mark it leaves on the ship -> the mark. When a choice makes a trail more dramatic vs keeps it a pure function of the Graph's true state -> the true state.* Corollaries, each of which becomes a rule or an acceptance line rather than a mechanic: (1) a trail is never drawn where the Graph has no descriptor, and a consequence-channel descriptor with sev >= 1 is never left undrawn on screen at the current zoom step (FT-3, FT-19); (2) the thickest point of every trail is its source, so following the gradient always ends at the failing fitting, seam, opening or rib (FT-3); (3) a trail stays on a floor, wall face, seam, opening frame or fitting, never on a machine body sprite, a gauge or a crewmate (FT-24); (4) the only way to remove a trail is to change the Graph (valve, patch, breaker, cut, warmth, time); no verb, toggle, setting, sanity stage or hallucination removes one (FT-2); (5) a trail is seen only by spending light, and in the dark the hiss, the cold and the draught still tell (FT-26). *Worked application:* "add a soft cyan outline to a leaking pipe so players spot it at maximum zoom-out" is a marker, not a mark, and adds a world-space highlight layer the art bible rejected; **rejected**. If legibility fails at the ladder floor the fix lives in the stamp itself (the 50 % legibility gate, FT-34), never in an added layer.

**Under hallucination the fantasy deepens rather than breaks.** Each sanity stage raises the *value* of the trail instead of eroding it. The ladder below is the table of record for the Player Fantasy; each row cites the rule that already guarantees it.

| Sanity drift stage (concept, Visual Identity Anchor) | What lies | What the trail does | The player learns | Guaranteed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Instruments disagree | gauge needle, inspect card | Unchanged; the gauge's physical witness (the Graph's LS-22 table, `LSG:205`) is still drawn when lit | "check the pipe, not the needle" | the consequence-channel list (`LSG:168`); In-World Instruments pairs with this system as the truth check (provisional, `IDX:39`) |
| 2. The light lies (the cone looks longer, phantom shadows) | the Filter's colour pass over the perceived cone | Trails are lit only by the **true** reveal radius (Lighting, a truth channel per the kernel's non-claim, `KERN:44`); beyond it the floor stays dark even where the cone *seems* to reach | "if the frost isn't lit, my light isn't there": the trail falsifies the light lie | FT-26; `PIPE:206` mask (provisional on Lighting) |
| 3. Loop hues bleed violet at pipe silhouettes (`ART:232`, `ART:238`) | pipe edges | The trail keeps its loop hue and contrast; violet on a pipe beside an unviolet frost fan is itself a tell | "the pipe is wrong, the frost is right" | the Filter is colour-only and truth-masked (`PIPE:206`); FT-2, FT-22 |
| Class (b), false system state | machine sprite whole or broken, gauge | The mark at the fitting is unchanged (ET-5) | "the floor outranks the machine" | FT-24 (anchored at the fitting, never on the body) |
| Class (a), phantom entity | a monster that is not there | A phantom tears nothing: it leaves no descriptor, so no mark. A **real** hidden entity that ruptures the hull leaves an ordinary BREACH or LEAK descriptor, drawn like any breach. Caveat in the Dependencies and Open Questions: a GOUGE on a non-hull subject surfaces only as the corruptible WORN descriptor, so "never the trail" is guaranteed for the hull, not yet for ducts and cables | "did it leave a mark?" is a check that costs a walk and light | FT-31; the routed Graph/Horror gap |
| Class (c), crewmate as horror | crewmate sprite | Not applicable to trails; the crewmate's lamp is the human channel (Lighting, Controller). Stated so this GDD does not reach for it | none | none (out of scope) |
| Class (d), phantom salvage | crate contents | A module's own fitting on a derelict may carry a dried trail from its authored dead fault: a cheap field read ("this one arced") that exists only where Derelict Authoring placed a dead fault | a candidate field check, never a guarantee | `LSG:226`; opportunity routed to Salvage and Derelict Authoring |

**Two non-negotiables (the guarantee half of the framing).** *The Filter may neither remove, alter nor add a trail* (`LSG:168`): there is no phantom trail, because a false trail would make the floor itself lie and no lie would then be falsifiable by the physical world, the one failure Pillar 4 forbids. And *this system is never a hallucination surface and has no per-player variant*: two crewmates looking at the same floor see the same marks, so co-op chatter moves from "is that scrubber green on your screen?" to "look at the floor" (the Fellowship contribution; the equal-extent promise depends on a routed Graph request, FT-33). **Cost of checking** (Pillar 4: "costs time, risk or trust"): reading a trail is never free. It costs light, a walk to where the lamp can reach, and time. The system must not reduce that cost with highlights, zoom-revealed hints or dark-visible marks.

**Reference techniques, taken precisely.**

| Reference | Adopt (exact technique) | Reject (exact technique) |
|---|---|---|
| Pandorum | the chest lamp as the only reader of a dark ship; the ship as the reliable narrator | none |
| Barotrauma | hull damage drawn where it is, as cracks on the wall you go to with a tool; a leak is a medium you see | the Status Monitor ship-overview screen; any per-room damage percentage |
| Dead Space (2008) | information lives on the world, not a HUD | the floor **locator line** pointing at the objective: a trail points to a source only because physics put it there, never to a goal |
| Return of the Obra Dinn | reconstructing cause from residue with no hint system (the verdict grammar, ET-4) | the confirmation ping when a deduction is right: no "correct" feedback |
| Project Zomboid | the room as a record; the base degrades on its own clock and noticing is competence | numeric condition readouts in context menus |
| Batman: Arkham, Witcher senses, any detective vision | none | any highlight, x-ray or scan mode that reveals trails or tints failing pipes: it turns reading into a button press and destroys "light is a decision" |
| Oxygen Not Included | gas and liquid as visible media, propagation shown in the medium | gas, temperature and power **overlays** (forbidden by `LSG:1003`) |
| Alien: Isolation | hissing pipes and leaking steam as the atmosphere of failure; checking costs you | **ambient decorative steam vents** that mean nothing: every mist here is a descriptor (no orphan trail, `ART:669`) |
| The Long Dark | cold read from the world before any number | **screen-space frost** on the view edge: body cues are Suit and HUD's, and this system draws nothing in screen space |
| FTL | causality between doors and air | room fire and breach **icons** (the icon layer the art bible rejected, `ART:44`-`ART:55`) |
| Dwarf Fortress fluids | propagation visible, not announced | per-tile simulation of the spread (the spatial gradient is presentation interpolation, `LSG:974`) |

**Tone and language (for every later document that names a trail).** Use the art bible's five behaviour words and the Graph's names: *mist drifts; frost fan, static, crystalline; scorch, sharp, radial, blooms; puddle, glossy, flat, ember edge (a stain when dried); hairline -> crack -> bent rib*; and *drip, weep, seam, bead, fitting, elbow, collar, lagging, residue*, with *still / live* and *dull / fresh* as the two axes. Avoid "smoke", "fog", "steam" (ambient, or the rejected perception mask); "rime" for coolant (branching rime is the hidden-entity footprint's word, `ART:565`); "burn mark", "explosion", "sparks shower" (combat register); "blood", "bleed", "pool of" anything bodily (flesh is sanity's hue, red is the alarm's); "damage decal", "health crack" (stat language); "leak indicator", "warning", "alert", "HP", "damage %" (meter language); "dead/active effect", "fades out" (a fade is presentation state); and "objective", "marker", "waypoint", "hint", "highlight" (guidance-system drift). Modes are always written `id NAME/MEDIUM` as the Graph does, and the count is always "six hazard groups, seventeen descriptor ids".

**What this GDD does not promise (anti-pillar guard rails; each traces to a pillar or anti-pillar and to the rule that enforces it).**
1. **No guidance system.** No marker, outline, ping, highlight, scan mode, objective line, world-space icon or arrow. A trail exists only where a descriptor exists, in the art bible's vocabulary (P1 design test; FT-3, FT-4).
2. **No meter, readout or alarm.** No numeric leak rate, hp tint, health bar, ship-status, schematic or minimap; never the alarm colour; `alarm_eligible` constrains this system only negatively (never first). UI Requirements: none; the three diegetic surfaces are the Graph's, and this system adds no surface (`LSG:1003`; FT-4).
3. **No lie on the floor.** No phantom trail, no "perceived" variant, no per-player trail, no sanity-driven change to any trail property (P4; FT-2).
4. **No repair reward.** No "fixed!" sparkle, completion ping, fade-out flourish or satisfaction effect. After a repair the descriptor is gone and the stamp is gone the next frame; the reward is the absence. **A patch leaves no trail:** the Graph clears the LEAK mode at the patch and a patched subject then carries only the WORN descriptor (`medium` NONE, `LSG:127`, `LSG:222`), which lives on the corruptible module-body channel (sprite wear, gauge, inspect card), so the floor goes quiet while the machine stays marked. This is the Graph's contract and is honoured literally; its Pillar 2 consequence is routed (FT-32). (`LSG:50` "no completion ping"; FT-1.)
5. **No combat feedback.** No hit-flash, damage number, impact decal for tool use against horrors, or "blood". A horror's own marks are web grammar, owned by Horror Entities (anti-pillar "not a combat game"; FT-4, FT-31).
6. **No light, and no readability in the dark.** A trail is a lit mark; it casts no light; the emissive layer on scorch, arc and ember is the only glow and it lights nothing (P3; FT-26, FT-27). Whether a fault's glow counts toward the web's exposure is decided by Emitter State's EM-17 (designed): the derived FAULT_GLOW emitter counts as exposure, the overlay does not.
7. **No cleaning, painting or decorating.** No verb, tool or setting erases or places a trail; welding is functional. Derelict authoring may not place loop-vocabulary decals that are not Graph faults (dead faults are Graph faults, `LSG:226`) (anti-pillar "not a freeform builder"; FT-3).
8. **No story beats.** Trails are not authored narrative props; the verdict grammar emerges from authored Graph faults, never from scripted sequences (anti-pillar "no authored story").
9. **No progression or score.** No count of trails fixed and no repair achievement surfaced by this system (anti-pillar "no win state"; `LSG:52`).
10. **Accessibility never removes truth.** Reduced motion holds moving media on a frame (direction and density kept); reduce flashing holds arcs as a steady glow; neither hides a trail (`PIPE:847`; FT-28, FT-29).

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Every rule below describes what the player sees and when; node classes appear only in the Engine notes (the closing sub-heading of Visual/Audio Requirements) and the budget table (D.9), tagged as ADR candidates; the registry facts this system introduces are handed to the registry stage in `registry-candidates.yaml`, not duplicated here. Rule ids are `FT-n`; formulas are `D.n` in the Formulas section and are named, never restated, here; knobs are UPPER_SNAKE in Tuning Knobs. The system registers **no block, no step hook and no command**: the words "hook", "block" and "command" appear below only in sentences that say so. Registry constants are cited by their registered name and value (`TICK_RATE` 60, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10, `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3, `TILE_PX` 48, `V_WALK` 2 and `V_RUN` 3 px per tick); Graph-owned constants (`LEAK_HP`, `BREACH_HP`, `ALARM_SEV_MIN`, `A_HOLE_MAX`, `SEV_STEP`, `OPEN_END_RATE_PM`, `FREEZE_WARN_MC` and kin) are registered with source the Graph GDD (2026-10-27) and are cited by registered name and value; they are not this system's to change. A value this system needs from an undesigned neighbour or a Graph field the Graph has not defined is tagged **PROVISIONAL** with its owner; a value this system owns and no neighbour has fixed is tagged **[PLACEHOLDER: NAME]** with its registry candidate. Where the art-director or an advisor ruling changed a row of a table of record, the row was rewritten, not annotated.*

#### 1. Identity and channel

**FT-1 Statelessness.** Every visual property of every trail element is a pure function of `read_view` fields (`failures()`, `compartment()`, `node()` and `edge()`), Layout geometry and `present_time` (animation phase only). The system owns no timer, no fade, no grow counter, no smoothing accumulator, no "last seen" flag, no spawn-time stamp and no `Tween`; the only members that persist across a Graph step are pooled scene-node references and the per-step cache of the last `read_view` poll, which is a copy of truth and not state. The test: the whole trail set can be rebuilt bit-identically from one `ALL` notification and `present_time`. Control-manifest lint line: "this system has no block, no hook, no command, no light node, no `true_value()` call, and no member that survives a Graph step except pooled node references and the last poll." *Why:* the Pipeline's PR-8 rejects exactly a presentation-owned fade countdown on a trail: the moment a trail's visibility depends on presentation state it is no longer the truth channel every hallucination is checked against (`PIPE:198`; P1, P4).

**FT-2 Truth channel.** Every node this system creates carries the TRUTH_PX grade of the Filter's PerceptionTag (registry: `perception_tag`). It reads `SIM` only, never `PERCEPTION_OUT`; it never calls `true_value()` (the Perception Filter is the only non-diagnostic caller); it has no "perceived" variant and no per-player trail. The Perception Filter may neither remove, alter nor add a trail, and this system's pixels are bit-identical with the Filter pass on or off. It works with either mask route the Filter chooses (a second viewport silhouette or per-element decomposition) and does nothing different when the mask is unavailable: in that case the Filter disables its own pass (`PIPE:735`). No sanity stage, setting, verb or hallucination class changes any trail property. *Why:* P4 "no lie is unfalsifiable"; the closed consequence-channel list (`LSG:168`); PR-10 (`PIPE:206`).

**FT-3 Descriptor-only drawing; one trail per source.** Every drawn element sits at a descriptor's anchor on a floor, wall face, seam, opening frame or fitting. No descriptor, no element. A source (one descriptor, keyed by `subject_kind`, `subject_id` and anchor) produces **one continuous trail, thickest at the source and never appearing at a node without upstream evidence on the connecting edge**. Two descriptors with different `mode` on one anchor draw as two trails in descriptor order (ascending `subject_id`, then `mode`). No orphan trail can occur: ambient decorative mist, steam or decals that are not Graph faults are not drawn by this system and Derelict Authoring may not place them. *Why:* "an orphan trail cannot occur" (`LSG:970`-`LSG:977`; `ART:669`; P1).

**FT-4 What this system never draws.** No meter, schematic, minimap, loop summary, numeric leak rate, health bar, damage number, hit flash, icon layer, beacon or alarm colour; nothing on a crewmate (Suit and HUD own body cues); nothing on a machine body sprite, gauge or inspect card (the module-body channel, FT-24); no web-grammar footprint (Horror Entities); no sound, and no gating on sound (Adaptive Audio leads, `IDX:41`); nothing that shows or hides Attention (P3). *Why:* `LSG:1003`; `ART:44`-`ART:55`; the anti-pillars.

#### 2. Signal-to-visual contract

**FT-5 Signal roles.** The three descriptor fields each drive one visual property, as the Graph documents them: `sev` selects the stamp size and the behaviour band; `intensity_pm` selects **density** (frame count, drip cadence, mist coverage as an ordered dither of 2x2-px cells, never partial alpha); `since_steps` selects **extent** and **accumulation** (frost reach, scorch darkening, dullness). A sev 3+ accent at the anchor (a wet collar, a heavy frost collar, a deep scorch pit) is drawn regardless of flow, so a pipe at hp 3999 with a trickle reads "badly damaged, barely flowing" and not "big leak" (exception: a gas leak at zero flow has no pixels at all, FT-18). Because `intensity_pm` is bucketed to 100 permille by the Graph, a density change always coincides with an `F/` key (D.4). *Why:* `LSG:158`-`LSG:159` document these roles; the fairness gate counts `sev` (`LSG:144`), so size tracks the Graph-guaranteed tell (P2).

**FT-6 Size ladder.** `trail_size` (D.1): **s (48 base px) at sev 1-2, m (96) at sev 3, l (144) at sev 4**; sev 0 draws nothing. Inside s there are two sub-tiers, *trace* (sev 1) and *full* (sev 2), so a sev 1 -> 2 step is still visible without a fourth size. For the integrity behaviour the sizes are the art bible's progression: s = **hairline**, m = **crack**, l = **bent rib or hole** (sev 4 = `A_hole` >= 32, `LSG:365`). Size is never produced by scaling a sprite (FT-22); it is a different drawing at the same pixel density. Size is a function of `sev` only (the single exception is the PUDDLE descriptor, which has no defined `sev`, FT-30), so a size change always coincides with an `F/` or `N/` key and needs no polling. **PROVISIONAL exception (Graph owner; routed in the single FT-30 amendment):** a SEAM descriptor with `medium` != NONE and `intensity_pm` > 0 draws at the s-*trace* tier even at sev 0, so a physically leaking weld seam is never undrawn (P1); the Graph states a seam weep's physical rate from the step the seam exists, at full `hp` (D.2, `LSG:308`-`LSG:330`), but not the `sev` its descriptor carries, and a sev derived from the Graph's D.4 at `hp` >= `HP_OK` is 0. The exception is removed the day the Graph states the seam sev. The s/m/l semantics are **defined by this GDD** because the art bible names "the three sizes of 3.3 and 6.3" (`ART:1043`) and neither section defines them; the art-director ratified them with the trace/full amendment. Within one trail the chain tapers: stamp ordinal i from the source uses size index `max(1, size_idx - floor(i / TAPER_STAMPS))` (D.1; integer division), where `TAPER_STAMPS` **[PLACEHOLDER: `TAPER_STAMPS`]** (default 1) is the number of run stamps laid per size tier before the chain steps down one tier, so a larger knob tapers *slower*; the source is the thickest point. *Why:* m first appears at sev 3, which is `ALARM_SEV_MIN` 3 (`LSG:901`, Graph GDD), so the physical channel visibly escalates at or before the alarm ("alarm never first", LS-13 `LSG:144`, R17; P2); large stamps stay rare, which is the draw budget (`PIPE:616`).

**FT-7 Which size cells can be reached (finding, no new rule).** By the Graph's D.4 (`LSG:365`; worked example `LSG:381`, which gives hp 5000 -> sev 2 and a patched hp 6000 -> sev 1) a subject at `hp` just below `LEAK_HP` 4000 (`LSG:127`, Graph GDD) is sev 3 (`SEV_STEP` 1500); SEVERED is forced `sev = max(sev, 3)` (`LSG:367`, `LSG:240`). So a **live `1 LEAK` descriptor (GAS, COOLANT or FUEL), a `2 SHORT` and a forced `8 SEVERED` are always sev >= 3 at birth**: they draw at m or l and never at s. The s cells are reached only by pre-stage and ambient descriptors: `7 OVERLOAD` (sev 1-2), the `4 FREEZE` pre-stage (sev 1), the `5 BREACH` pre-stage hairline (sev 1-2, PROVISIONAL and pillar-critical for Pillar 2, see FT-32 and Open Questions 5), `3 OVERHEAT`'s HOT shimmer (sev 2), `20 ATMO_COLD` (sev 2), `26 SEAL_STRAIN` (sev 1), and seam weeps if the Graph gives them sev 1-2 (it states no seam-weep severity; PROVISIONAL). **Consequence for the asset list:** author s-frost, s-scorch, s-hairline and s-strain for the MVP; defer s-mist and s-puddle until the Graph states the seam-weep severity. *Why:* the pre-stage descriptors are the Graph's fairness tell (`LSG:127`); their trails are the s hairlines the player must learn to read.

**FT-8 Extent is a pure function, bounded twice.** The drawn length of a trail along its edge (D.2 `trail_extent`) is the **minimum of three things, never less than `MIN_EXTENT_PX`** (an m or l head stamp is revealed by D.10 like every other stamp, so at `since_steps` 0 a sev 4 head shows 48 of its 144 px, threshold 42 of 127, and a sev 3 head 48 of 96, threshold 63; this is the intended "it begins" read and not an undrawn source): the physical edge length; the **age bound** `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[sev] x since_steps` (clamped by `SINCE_CLAMP`); and the **temperature bound** `reach_px` (D.3 `frost_reach` for cold media, the whole edge for every other medium). All arithmetic is integer; the result is floored to base px. A live descriptor therefore always draws the first `MIN_EXTENT_PX` 48 [PLACEHOLDER: `MIN_EXTENT_PX`] base px of its source stamp on its first step. A severity rise is a one-frame jump of the age bound (up to the whole edge), which is intended ("it just got serious") and is not smoothed. **Render-rate smoothing is off by default** (`TRAIL_EXTENT_SMOOTH` false [PLACEHOLDER]); when on, it interpolates only between the extent at `since_steps - 1` and at `since_steps`, evaluated from the committed tick and `tick_alpha`, never extrapolating (`KERN:298`); it needs no stored previous value (D.2) and it lags the committed value by up to one Graph step (0.1 s). Because `since_steps` dirties no key (`LSG:532`), extent is read from the per-step poll cache each frame (FT-15). *Why:* a pure function is restore-safe, MIRROR-safe, reduced-motion-safe and testable; a presentation grow timer is the rejected alternative of PR-8 (FT-1).

**FT-9 Growth invariants.** `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[sev]` is an **integer** (base px per Graph step; the pipeline places by `floor`). It must satisfy: (i) `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[1]` >= 1, so a sev-1 trail crosses a six-tile room (288 base px) in at most 288 steps = 28.8 s, inside the 30 s tell ceiling the Pipeline routed to this system (`PIPE:1081`, item 15); (ii) **`V_WALK` 2 px/tick (12 px per Graph step) < `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[4]` <= `V_RUN` 3 px/tick (18 px per Graph step)**, so a sev-4 single-edge trail is strictly faster than walking and no faster than running; (iii) monotone non-decreasing in `sev`. Placeholder table **[PLACEHOLDER: `GROW_PX_PER_STEP`]**: sev 1 = 2, sev 2 = 3, sev 3 = 6, sev 4 = 15 px per step (20 / 30 / 60 / 150 px/s); a 288 px crossing then takes 144, 96, 48 and 20 steps (14.4, 9.6, 4.8 and 2.0 s). Strict reading flagged for tuning: at the 100-step fairness floor (`W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10) a pure sev-1 trail at 2 px per step has covered 200 px of 288, so invariant (i) holds but a "complete by 10 s" reading needs `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[1]` = 3 (300 px at 100 steps); the acceptance test will state which reading it checks (Open Questions). *Why:* "a failure trail propagating along a graph edge faster than you can walk" (`ART:224`); P2.

**FT-10 The cascade front owns the other half of the breach image.** The Graph lights successive edges one hop per run (`LSG:129`, LS-12; at most `MAX_SPREAD_PER_STEP` 32 new failures per run), each edge's trail starting at its own `since_steps` 0. **No propagation animation state exists in this system**; the front's speed is the Graph's. The breach image is therefore true twice: single-edge sev-4 growth (FT-9 ii) and the cascade front. The art-bible carried element is satisfied by both. *Why:* `ART:224`; scope guard against absorbing the Graph.

**FT-11 The coolant frost fan is unconditional; its reach is not.** A `1 LEAK/COOLANT` descriptor always carries a static crystalline frost fan (a local stamp at the anchor and on the lagging) **at every temperature**, plus the drip; it draws **no drifting mist** (mist is O2's word). The fan's *spread* along the edge and over the room's surfaces is bounded by the temperature bound of FT-8: D.3 `frost_reach` maps the room's true `T` (polled from `compartment()`) to a maximum reach, with knots at the band edges [PLACEHOLDER: `FROST_REACH_PX`] (COMFORT and warmer: local stamp only; COOL/FROST edge: long; FROST/FREEZING edge: longer; FREEZING/DEEP_COLD edge: the whole edge) and **linear interpolation inside a band** [PLACEHOLDER: `FROST_REACH_INTERP` true; false restores a pure per-band step table], so frost stops growing when the band leaves FROST and retreats continuously as the heater warms the bay, with no stored state (the cryo-bay case, `LSG:839`). **Knob constraint (fixture floor):** with interpolation the reach at the cryo-bay fixture band sits near the warm end of FROST, so `FROST_REACH_PX` must satisfy `frost_reach(T = -2000 mC, large since_steps)` >= 336 px (the far wall of `ART:142`, seven tiles); otherwise the far-wall frost of `ART:142` / `LSG:839` is unreachable whatever `fail_since_step` the fixture authors. The placeholder knots give 544 px at -2000 mC (D.3). **Room frost with no leak** (`20 ATMO_COLD`, row 20) is anchored at the compartment's coldest consequence-channel anchor (a COOLANT/HEAT descriptor) if one exists, else at the OPENINGs carrying `AIRFLOW`/`SEAL_STRAIN` and the exposed hull tiles (`room_surface`, provisional), and is bounded by the same D.3 reach. *(orchestrator ruling: the Graph's informative map reads "LEAK/COOLANT -> drip + mist, frost below 0 C" (`LSG:166`), and the Graph itself calls the map Failure & Trail's; shape redundancy outranks an informative table, because the colourblind backbone "O2 = drifting mist, coolant = static crystalline" (`ART:476`) must hold on the home-ship at COMFORT, where the game spends most of its time. Both clauses of the Graph's parenthetical, "+ mist" and "frost below 0 C", are therefore not rendered as written; a one-line informative rewording is routed to the Graph owner, non-blocking. Interpolation inside a band avoids a retreat jump of up to 480 px at a band key; the toggle exists so the literal per-band table can be restored.)* *Why:* P1 greyscale and colourblind redundancy (`ART:47`, `ART:314`, `ART:476`).

**FT-12 Hue from the loop, shape from the medium; the verdict grammar.** Each descriptor's colour is its **loop's hue** and its shape is its **medium's behaviour word**: mist (O2 loop), frost (coolant loop and cold surfaces), scorch (power), puddle (fuel), hairline -> crack -> bent rib (integrity). `medium` HEAT (OVERHEAT and SCRAM) has no behaviour row in the art bible and takes the hue of the loop the subject sits on (a scorch on a power load or reactor is rimmed in the power hue, on a coolant reservoir in the coolant hue, on a fuel tank in the fuel hue); no heat hue is invented. Compartment descriptors carry no loop: `20 ATMO_COLD` frost uses the coolant-ice hue because frost is the material of cold (shape, not loop, carries the meaning); `21 ATMO_HOT` soot and `24 AIRFLOW` dust are hue-less. Every behaviour carries **both contrast polarities** (a light element and a dark element) so it reads on lit and mid floor tones; scorch is a dark radial core with a loop-hue rim. The five behaviours stay distinct in greyscale and in a still frame: *mist drifts* (light, round clusters, no straight edge, no angle); *frost is static and crystalline* (lightest, hex-stair spurs branching at 60 degrees, no curve); *scorch is sharp and radial* (dark, straight spokes from one core, uneven lengths, an origin); *a puddle is glossy and flat* (dark, one convex blob, glint specks, no spokes); *a hairline runs along a rib or seam, never from a point* (mid plus dark pair, at most one fork per 16 px). Frost has one dendrite motif in three layouts: a **fan** (radial, at a coolant fitting or lagged elbow: `1 LEAK/COOLANT`), a **crust** (a linear run along an edge: `20 ATMO_COLD`, hatch rim frost, a dried coolant run) and a **sheath** (a continuous thick wrap over a pipe's lagging: `4 FREEZE`), so a fan at a fitting is coolant, a crust with no fitting is cold, and a sheath with no drip and no fan origin is a frozen pipe. **Scorch with a radial origin is a power arc; soot without an origin is heat** (`21 ATMO_HOT` draws a soot wash, not a scorch). Palette, reserved hues and exact values are in Visual/Audio. *Why:* `ART:314` (five behaviours), `ART:697` (verdict grammar), `ART:309` and `ART:399` (scorch dark, in its hue), checklist 13 (`ART:1216`, a trail out-contrasts every floor it crosses).

**FT-13 Wall mark plus floor run: one trail.** A descriptor on a wall-mounted fitting renders one continuous trail with a **wall part** (the Y-sorted wall layer, at the host face's sort origin) and a **floor part** (the floor-marks band) whose start point and direction Layout supplies (provisional `anchor_geometry`). Both parts share the single extent of FT-8, measured along the combined polyline, wall part first. Wall cells are 48, 96 and 144 px wide and at most 72 px tall (a wall front face is 72 px, `ART:1033`); floor cells are square. No opaque trail pixel may lie outside its own compartment's floor tiles or its host face: head and source stamps may fill a full cell, body stamps are at most a 48 px lateral strip at m and l, and the Layout clearance read is provisional (FT-37). *Why:* "one trail per source" (FT-3) and the eye line trail -> source -> hatch (`ART:704`).

**FT-14 Seam weep along the bead.** A SEAM subject's descriptor renders the medium's trail **along the raised Hull Grey weld bead** in the Y-sorted layer at the seam's host-face origin: mist along the bead for GAS, rim frost for HEAT, a bowed rim for SEAL_STRAIN on a seam hatch; its extent runs along the seam line per FT-8. The Graph states a seam weep's physical rate (`A_seam` >= 1 per tile for an imperfect weld, `LSG:308`) but **not its descriptor mode, medium or severity** (PROVISIONAL, routed with the nine ids of FT-30): until it does, the seam row assumes mode `1 LEAK`, medium GAS and a sev derived from the weep as for any LEAK-class descriptor; if the Graph emits sev 1-2 the s-mist cell becomes reachable (FT-7). *Why:* the weld is hero shape #3 and "the ship's first failure point"; the carried element "a failure trail spreading along a weld seam while you watch" (`ART:166`); `LSG:228` must-prove (P5, P2).

#### 3. Time, keys and reconcile

**FT-15 Keys wake, polls move.** A notification handler stores the key set and returns; it never reads or draws (`PIPE:190`, PR-8 rule 3). In the next render callback the system (1) re-reads `failures()` and `compartment()` (and, for the provisional rows of FT-30, `node()` and `edge()`, whose continuous `flow` and machine `T` dirty no key) **once per Graph step** into the per-step poll cache (the Graph runs once per `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6 ticks = 0.1 s; `since_steps` dirties no key, `LSG:532`), (2) spawns, despawns and re-selects size and behaviour on `F/` and `N/`, band accents on `B/` and `C/`, and rebuilds anchors on `T` and `SEC/`, and (3) every frame reads extent, dullness and animation phase from the poll cache and `present_time`. The key-family reaction table is below. *Why:* LS-20 (`LSG:201`) says keys wake discrete presentation only; PR-8 rule 3 and the kernel's CR-10.

**FT-16 `ALL` reconciles in one frame.** On `ALL` (a restore, a derelict instantiation, or a cascade above `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, `KERN:466`) the system performs **one full re-read reconciled against its node pool within the same render frame**, never spread over frames. `TRAIL_RECONCILE_BUDGET_MS` [PLACEHOLDER, 2 ms] is a **logging threshold, not a cut-off**, exactly as `STEP_BUDGET_MS` (4 ms) is for the kernel. After a restore the trail set appears already grown, because extent is a function of `since_steps` and nothing "starts" on load. The consumer-side cost is D.7 `trail_reconcile_cost`, which cites the registry formula `dirty_key_budget` by name and never restates its expression. *Why:* the MVP worst case is about 38 keys and about 17 descriptors (`LSG:532`), so one-frame reconciliation is cheap and keeps "never suppressed" (FT-19) testable.

**FT-17 LIVE / STILL and dullness are two orthogonal axes.** **LIVE vs STILL** is `intensity_pm` > 0 vs = 0 (for the nine PROVISIONAL ids, the per-row LIVE predicate of FT-30 and D.4 in its place): STILL stops the drip and the moving-medium frames; the static stamps stay and draw at the authored coverage of their size for the current `sev` (the system remembers no earlier density, FT-1, so "held" means "does not animate", not "recalled"). **Dullness** is a quantised, palette-locked ramp of `since_steps` (D.5 `dullness`, `DULL_STEPS` [PLACEHOLDER], `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` [PLACEHOLDER]) applied to **static stamps only**: a frost fan goes from bright to a greyed fan, a scorch from rimmed to matte, a puddle to a stain. Moving media (drip, mist, debris) are never dulled, so a live leak keeps looking wet. There is **no third "DRIED" state**: a derelict's authored dead fault (`intensity_pm` 0, large `since_steps`, `LSG:226`) and a home-ship leak isolated by a closed valve (`intensity_pm` 0, mode kept, `LSG:127`) obey the same STILL rule. This system cannot know *when* a leak was isolated, because `since_steps` counts from `fail_since_step`; an old isolated leak therefore looks old, which is honest. Fuel is the exception by medium: the pool follows `fuel_puddle_ml` (row 25), shrinking only as the Graph's puddle does, and the fuel stain's dullness is a **palette swap with no intermediate stop and no dither**, because any mix of the fuel hue with a grey passes through the flesh band the sane world reserves for sanity (Visual/Audio). `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` must be at least 7500 steps (12.5 min, the longest live MVP clock, `LSG:667` D.15) so no live trail reaches the dried look before its fault finishes. *Why:* three discrete states would need a presentation timer to tell STOPPED from DRIED (FT-1); the art bible chose one shader with state from the mask (`ART:1065`-`ART:1067`).

| Axis state | `intensity_pm` | `since_steps` | Draws |
|---|---|---|---|
| LIVE, fresh | > 0 | small | stamp chain, moving media at `density_level`, full-colour static stamps |
| LIVE, aged | > 0 | large | as above, static stamps dulled by D.5; the drip still wet (the moving medium is never dulled) |
| STILL, fresh | 0 | small | stamp chain only, full colour; the valve was closed moments ago ("the drip stops, the frost stops growing") |
| STILL, aged | 0 | large | stamp chain only, dulled; an old isolated leak |
| STILL, dried (derelict) | 0 | authored very large | the verdict: greyed fan, matte scorch, ember-dim stain; **GAS rows have no STILL form** (FT-18) |

**FT-18 A gas leak at zero flow is empty by design.** `1 LEAK/GAS` (and `8 SEVERED/GAS`) at `intensity_pm` 0 draws **zero pixels**: "O2 failure leaves no trail (mist evaporates), read from absence" (`ART:697`), and no residue accent exists for GAS (a stain would suggest a false O2 history on a derelict). A sev-3 trickle therefore reads "wide, sparse plume". This GDD **reads the Graph's lint** (`LSG:1110`, which scans for a consumer that "suppresses a consequence-channel descriptor whose sev >= 1 from its physical channel") as: *no consequence-channel descriptor with `medium` != NONE and sev >= 1 may be hidden from its physical channel while it has flow; an empty STILL GAS plume is not suppression, because the physical flow is zero.* The reading is this GDD's, not the Graph's, and is listed in Open Questions as routed to the Graph owner (non-blocking). *Why:* the verdict grammar (P5); `ART:697`.

**FT-19 Never cull an on-screen consequence-channel element.** Every descriptor with `medium` != NONE and sev >= 1 whose anchor lies inside the camera field **at the current ladder step k** has its stamp drawn that frame (subject to FT-18). Budget pressure degrades moving media first, in this order: frame count, then density, then `TRAIL_ANIM_FPS` halving, then far-from-beam frame hold; **never stamps**. Not drawing is permitted only for off-field anchors (re-evaluated at every k, so a zoom-out reveals every trail), dormant sections (they emit no descriptors, `LSG:822`) and sev 0. Distance culling and darkness culling are forbidden: they are exactly the suppression the Graph's lint scans for (`LSG:1110`). *Why:* P1 via `LSG:144` R17 (no consumer may suppress a consequence-channel descriptor's physical channel).

**FT-20 Pure variation.** Stamp variant, drip phase offset and fan flip come from `rng_derive(world_seed, "trail.vis", (subject_id << 20) | stamp_index)` (D.6), the pure hash the kernel defines (`KERN:470`, D.10); the kernel's H9 restricts *simulation* from wall-clock and global randomness, and a presentation caller of a pure hash violates nothing. `world_seed` is read as exposed through **the kernel's read view of `kernel.clock`** (`KERN:477`, `KERN:844`; CR-12 makes every `SIM` block readable by presentation; the confirmation that `kernel.clock` is registered class `SIM` is routed to the kernel owner, non-blocking). If that read is not available, the fallback `TRAIL_VIS_SEED_FALLBACK` 0 [PLACEHOLDER] is identical on every client and after every restore, only less varied. Engine global random functions and any random-number-generator instance are forbidden in this system (lint). *Why:* two crewmates must see the same frost fan (P1); a restore redraws the same trail (`LSG:829`).

#### 4. Look: technique, bands, pixels

**FT-21 Static stamps and stepped frames.** Static marks (frost fan, crust and sheath, scorch, glossy streak, puddle, hairline, crack and bent rib, bowed rim, rim frost, soot wash, and any dulled form) are **stamps** from the three-size sheets. Moving media (mist drift, drip, vent debris, wind streaks, AIRFLOW dust, arc pops, shimmer) are **pooled stepped sprite frames** at `TRAIL_ANIM_FPS` 12 [PLACEHOLDER] (the horror-rig precedent of 12 fps stepped, `PIPE:202`), with frame index from `present_time` (D.8), never from wall-clock or the tick counter (PR-8 rule 6). Particle systems are permitted **only** for venting at a BREACH or AIRFLOW opening, and only after a spike proves (a) per-instance positions floor to the base grid and (b) alpha is binary under nearest sampling (both unverified, HIGH risk); until then venting is stepped frames too. The "propagation frames" the art bible lists for the trail sheet are not needed and are dropped from the asset list: a growing front is a reveal threshold (FT-23), not extra drawings. *Why:* binary alpha and integer placement are exactly where particles are weakest; stepped frames satisfy both by construction (FT-22).

**FT-22 Pixel integrity.** Nearest sampling; every item at an integer base-px position (placed by `floor`); **no per-node scale animation of any kind** (growth is stamp count, size swap and the reveal threshold of FT-23); `rotation` 0; hairlines, filaments and seams are **sprites, never runtime line draws** (PR-4 rule 10; the engine's 4.7 line-antialiasing change is moot for exactly this reason); alpha is binary (0 or 255) on every drawn sprite, including mist (a partially transparent mist is not pixel art); palette lock; minimum feature 2x2 px, except that a hairline is a **1 px Hull Grey run paired with a 1 px Void Steel shadow line (a 2 px composite, at least 4 px long)**, which the art-director ratified as a permitted 1 px element joining contour and glyph strokes, an amendment candidate to the art bible's minimum-feature checklist (routed); no single-pixel noise; no violet and no flesh hue in any trail sheet or any dulled render; the alarm colour, the glowstick light colour and human (Tungsten) light appear in no trail. *Why:* `PIPE:110`-`PIPE:123`; `ART:1088`; `ART:422`-`ART:430`.

**FT-23 Chain, reveal by threshold, density by dither.** A trail is an **anchor cap** at the source, **run** pieces chained along the polyline, a **tip** at the leading edge, **corner** pieces where Layout turns the line, and a **terminal** piece when the extent reaches its bound. The last run piece is revealed up to the extent by comparing its flow-phase ramp channel with an integer threshold (so a frost front is ragged and a scorch front radial with no extra art), never by scaling. Density (FT-5) is a **world-anchored dither of 2x2-px cells**, never partial alpha: the pattern keys on world base-px coordinates so it does not swim with the camera, overlapped stamps agree and every client draws the same pixels. Mechanism (runtime discard by coverage rank vs pre-authored dither sets) is an ADR candidate. *Why:* PR-4 rules 4 and 10; `ART:1065`-`ART:1067`.

**FT-24 Never on a machine body, never on a crewmate.** OVERHEAT and SCRAM scorch, shimmer and glow are drawn at the **descriptor's anchor port or fitting** and on the floor and wall marks around the machine, never as an effect on the machine's body sprite (the corruptible module-body channel). Shimmer is a stepped frame sprite at the fitting; this system has no distortion shader of any kind. A trail is anchored to the fitting or edge, never to a machine's body, so a class (b) hallucination that redraws the body cannot move or hide the mark. *Why:* `LSG:168` (a lie can live only on the body channel); P4.

**FT-25 Draw bands.**

| Element | Band (Pipeline name) | z | Y-sorted | Sort origin |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Floor trails and puddles: frost run and crust, floor scorch, glossy streak, puddle, floor-plate cracks, soot edges | Floor marks | +1 | never | none: drawn flat, order among marks = authoring order; a mark never sorts above an actor standing on it and never below the hull it sits on |
| Wall trails and frame sprites: frost fan on the lagging or elbow, wall scorch, crack and rib along the wall face, hatch rim sprites, soot on lower faces | Wall layer (the one Y-sorted layer) | +10 | yes | the host face's sort origin, so the trail sorts with its face |
| Moving media: mist, drip, vent debris, wind streaks, shimmer, AIRFLOW dust | Dust and particles | +30 | no | "venting mist above a trail, below nothing" |
| Emissive layer (scorch, arc, ember) | shares its host stamp's band | as host | as host | as host |
| Not used | Floor (z 0), wall caps (z +20) | | | |

All bands stay inside every light range limit (`PIPE:132`-`PIPE:139`). Placement by `floor`; all marks sit under the hull-floor material, so an unlit trail never renders below the hull it sits on (`ART:1248`). *Why:* PR-5 band table; `PIPE:773` (hard dependency: layer assignment and nearest sampling that keeps a hairline a hairline).

**FT-26 Lit albedo marks.** Trails are lit marks under the hull-floor material (`max(albedo x ambient, floor tint)`); only scorch, arc and ember carry an emissive layer. In a dark room the trail is not seen, and that is correct: the hiss (Audio), the band cues (Suit) and the draught still tell. Acceptance captures are taken **lit**; the dark-room criterion asserts the descriptor and its acoustic class exist while no *non-emissive* trail pixel exceeds the hull-floor tint (a live emissive element may be visible and is the subject of FT-27). *Why:* "darkness is honest; light is a decision" (P1, P3); `ART:1248`.

**FT-27 No light and no emitter registration.** This system writes no light node and registers no emitter and no exposure. It supplies a **descriptor-to-glow data table** (palette hex, radius in base px, flicker class bounded by FT-29) in Visual/Audio; Emitter State registers the emitter from the same descriptor and Lighting renders it (designed; EM-17). The emissive layer on scorch, arc and ember stamps is an unshaded additive layer that **casts no light**. Whether a visible fault glow counts toward the web's exposure is not an art or presentation claim; it is decided by Emitter State's **EM-17**: "The emissive `_msk` trail glow on a sprite is an *unshaded overlay, not an emitter*, and exposes nothing", while the FAULT_GLOW *emitter* that `emitter.place` derives from the same descriptor "counts as exposure". This GDD supplies the ledger values Emitter State's D.11 multiplies (`GLOW_BASE_PM[key]` and `SEV_GLOW_PM[0..4]`, V/A.6; `EM:543`). *(CP-3, a light written here would illuminate a room without being an emitter the Attention system can see, so the narrower contract wins; EM-17 decides that the derived FAULT_GLOW emitter counts as exposure and the overlay does not.)* *Why:* P3 "emission is exposure".

**FT-28 Reduced motion.** Reduced motion holds every moving medium on its **authored hold frame** (frame 0 of each stepped strip; direction and density kept, so a mist that does not drift is still a mist), while extent, size, density and dullness continue because they are Graph state; `present_time` still advances. It never suppresses a trail, a hairline or audio. Each hold frame must read as its behaviour word in a still image (mist = peak-coverage frame with one wisp leaning along `dir`; drip = a hanging bead with a 2 px streak below it; vent debris = a mid-flight cluster; AIRFLOW dust = directional streaks with a tapered tail; arc = a steady glow at 60 percent of peak with no pops; shimmer = a static three-line wave). *Why:* `PIPE:232`, `PIPE:847`: reduced motion "never suppresses trails, tremor, hairlines or audio".

**FT-29 Reduce flashing and the arc number.** Arc and ember flicker is a **number**: `ARC_FLICKER_PER_S` [PLACEHOLDER] <= `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3, counted per `flash_count` and `PIPE:228` (opposing >= 10 % luminance changes per rolling 1 s window). With several arcs in one region (a ninth of the displayed picture; ~213x120 base px at the default 640 x 360 field) the budget is shared (D.8b `arc_flash_share`: effective rate = floor(`FLASH_MAX_PER_S` / n_arcs); 0 means a held glow), and the emissive layer's luminance swing is listed in Visual/Audio so the regional clamp can be checked. "Reduce flashing" replaces flicker by a held glow. The light verbs are fixed so a decoy, a flare and an arc cannot be confused: **arcs pop**, deployables flicker, the cryo lamp gutters, pipes glow, fuel edges glint (a static highlight). *Why:* `PIPE:228` PR-13; `ART:979`; `ART` 3.2 and 2.2 reservations.

#### 5. Provisional rows, footprints, co-op, scale

**FT-30 Nine descriptor ids and several fields are PROVISIONAL.** The Graph defines `intensity_pm` only for leak rate, hole area and AIRFLOW (`LSG:158`), SEVERED (`OPEN_END_RATE_PM` 500 permille of port cap, `LSG:772`) and SEAL_STRAIN (`min(1000, |P_a - P_b|)`, `LSG:649`). For `2 SHORT`, `7 OVERLOAD`, `3 OVERHEAT`, `9 SCRAM`, `4 FREEZE` and `20-23 ATMO_*` it defines none, so those rows drive density from `sev` (`density_level` = `sev` in D.4), age from `since_steps` and the relevant true value from `node()` or `compartment()` (loading or flow for electric, `T` for heat and cold); for these nine ids LIVE is not `intensity_pm` > 0 (the Graph defines none) but a **per-row LIVE predicate on a true value** read through `node()`, `edge()` or `compartment()` (D.4: `2 SHORT` live while the faulted conductor carries current, `3 OVERHEAT` live while the machine's `T` is above the Graph's OVERHEAT_OFF edge, cited by name; every other provisional id has no moving medium and is always drawn static), so a `2 SHORT` pops while current flows and goes still when the breaker opens, whatever the Graph's `intensity_pm` would have been; the static stamp always draws (FT-19) and only the moving media follow the predicate (PROVISIONAL, folded into the single Graph request below). This GDD never states a formula for the Graph's `intensity_pm`. One narrow amendment request is routed to the Graph owner, listing exactly: the nine ids; the **provenance of `since_steps` for non-node subjects** (COMPARTMENT, OPENING and SEAM descriptors have no stated `fail_since_step`); the seam-weep descriptor's mode, medium and severity (FT-14); `sev` and a magnitude for `25 PUDDLE` and `sev` for `24 AIRFLOW`; the `loop` that an OVERHEAT or SCRAM descriptor carries (FT-12); and the `medium` carried by a frozen non-coolant tank (a frozen fuel tank must not show coolant's frost fan as its identity). *(CD-GDD-ALIGN, stage 12; three pillar-level asks folded into this same single amendment, none restating a Graph value:)* (a) **a LEAK-class sev-1 pre-stage descriptor on a patched subject at `hp` < `HP_OK`**, which this system then draws by the existing `1 LEAK` row as a weeping fan (FT-32, Open Questions 4); (b) **confirmation that the `5 BREACH` pre-stage at `hp` < `HP_OK` is a mode-5 STRUCT descriptor at sev 1-2 and not WORN, and the seam-weep severity**, pillar-critical for Pillar 2 (FT-32, Open Questions 5); (c) **that the flow-zero `5 BREACH` pre-stage descriptor carries the `acoustic` class TICK**, the hull groan the Graph's LS-22 Hull row already names as a consequence-channel witness (`LSG:215`; TICK is in the `acoustic` enumeration, `LSG:163`), so the hull-fatigue tell has a non-visual cue where the hairline does not resolve (FT-34). *Why:* inventing a Graph field's semantics in a consumer is owner absorption.

**FT-31 Footprints are Graph descriptors only.** The system draws nothing for a footprint as such. A hull RUPTURE or GOUGE (M17 `deposit_trace`) surfaces as ordinary hp loss and a BREACH or LEAK descriptor and is drawn exactly like any breach, **along the rib or seam** whatever caused it; there is no "exogenous" variant. *(CP-2, the radial EXOGENOUS variant is withdrawn because `ART:565` reserves radiating hairlines to the footprint grammar. Should a radial look ever be wanted for a RUPTURE it carries a Void Steel hole centre and Hull Grey lips, and Horror's footprint stays hue-less and holeless.)* The Graph persists no footprint record (`LSG:196`, `LSG:912`); CHILL and BURN (at most `FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q` 20000 dJ, `LSG:196`, Graph GDD) rarely change a band and STRESS has no descriptor, so those three kinds have no trail (routed to the Graph owner as a VS-A candidate: a read-only footprint mark). Writes above `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` (16) are dropped by the Graph, so a trail never shows a footprint the Graph did not accept (neither the registry's "per subject" nor the Graph's "in one run" scope wording is restated). **Known truth-channel hole (routed):** a GOUGE on a duct or cable surfaces only as the WORN descriptor (module-body, corruptible) or as a LEAK, contradicting "the hallucination hides the body, never the trail" for non-hull subjects; until the Graph owner closes it, **MVP Horror restricts GOUGE to hull segments; this restriction is binding on the Horror Entities GDD, not advisory** (creative-director ruling, CD-GDD-ALIGN stage 12). **Joint guarantee:** Pillar 4(a), "the hallucination hides the body, never the trail", is guaranteed jointly by this GDD (Graph descriptors, hull subjects) and by Horror Entities (web-grammar footprints: sound, frost, hull stress, light disturbance), so neither document may assume the other covers every footprint kind. Web-grammar marks (wet iridescent smear, branching rime, dust direction, filament twitch) are Horror's and share the floor-marks band; this system never draws coolant-fan-looking rime and tests for contrast collisions with Horror's marks. *Why:* `ART:565`; P4(a).

**FT-32 A patched subject draws nothing (the Graph's contract, honoured literally); the BREACH pre-stage is PROVISIONAL.** A patch raises `hp` to at most `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000, which is above `LEAK_HP` 4000, so the Graph clears the LEAK mode and the patched subject carries **only the WORN descriptor** (mode 10, `medium` NONE, `acoustic` NONE) until `PATCH_DECAY_S` 240 wears it down (`LSG:222`, `LSG:127`). WORN is the module-body channel, so this system draws nothing for it: the trail vanishes the frame the patch lands, a change the player caused. *(CP-1, the Graph's own definition of WORN wins over the flavour text "a patch weeps"; a patch is a change the player caused, and anti-promise 4 states the consequence.)* **Consequence routed to the Graph owner (truth-channel hole):** after the decay the re-leak is preceded only by WORN descriptors (hp falling toward `LEAK_HP`), a corruptible channel, while Pillar 2 wants a consequence-channel tell for it. **Ruling in force (CD-GDD-ALIGN, stage 12; Pillar 2 and Pillar 4's "at least one channel of truth remains"):** a body-only tell fails Pillar 2's design test on the consequence channel, so this GDD **requests** a LEAK-class sev-1 pre-stage descriptor for patched subjects at `hp` < `HP_OK` (folded into the single FT-30 Graph amendment; this system then draws it by the existing LEAK row as a weeping fan and the s cells become reachable). **The literal reading above stays in force only until the Graph owner rules**; no Graph value is restated or changed here. The same ambiguity affects the hull: `LSG:127` calls the BREACH pre-stage "the sev-1 hairline at hp < HP_OK" while also defining WORN for any `hp` < `HP_OK` with no mode 1-9 live; this GDD **assumes a mode-5 STRUCT descriptor at sev 1-2 with `intensity_pm` 0** (row 5, PROVISIONAL), and if the Graph emits WORN there, the hull hairline falls to the corruptible channel and the Pillar 2 tell for hull fatigue is lost. **This is pillar-critical (Open Questions 5 is blocking for implementation of row 5):** the creative direction recommends the Graph confirm the mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage and the seam-weep severity; the FT-6 sev-0 seam exception is kept, because it correctly protects Pillar 1, until the seam severity is confirmed and then retired. *Why:* `LSG:127`, `LSG:222` are locked; a stale reading would give the consistency check a conflict.

**FT-33 MIRROR degradation (VS-B).** The co-op summary carries active descriptors as `(id, mode, sev, alarm_eligible)` only (`LSG:203`); it has neither `fail_since_step` nor `intensity_pm`. Until the Graph owner adds them (or the 100-permille bucket; under 8 bytes per active descriptor against the ~0.4 KB summary), a MIRROR client **degrades to sev-only**: size from `sev`, density level fixed at the sev default, extent = the whole edge. This is a named known VS-B gap, not an MVP concern (MVP is single-player). *Why:* the kernel's CR-10 requires identical presentation in HOST and MIRROR; P1.

**FT-34 Zoom invariance and budgets at every ladder step.** Layout is in base px and identical at every integer zoom step k in [1, `k_auto` + 2] (`PIPE:161`, C6). Budgets (D.9 `trail_budget_at_k`) are stated **at every k and at 1080p, 1440p and 2160p, not at `k_auto` alone**: at k = 1 the camera field is up to 9x (1080p), 16x (1440p) or 36x (2160p) the area of the 640x360 default, so far more trails are on screen at once. The Pipeline's draw-call and particle-emitter caps are restated by name in Visual/Audio and Section D: floor marks `D_marks` 4-20 draws, at most 12 live particle emitters (mist at most 64 per trail, venting at most 128 per vent; "emitter" alone is Emitter State's record vocabulary, EM-3 and EM-4) re-expressed as frame-sprite counts for the stepped path, one Y-sorted layer of 50-300 draws, whole-frame 1000 draws (`PIPE:616`, `ART:1173`); if the sorted layer exceeds ~300 draws the remedy is per-row z-index bands for wall faces, never fewer props. Legibility gate: full legibility at `k_auto` and the first zoom-out; at k = 1 only polarity, hue blob and form class are guaranteed for m and l, and a sev 1-2 hairline (a 2 px composite) may not resolve, which is accepted and logged (not a licence to cull, FT-19, nor to add markers). **The non-visual cue that carries the flow-zero hull pre-stage at k = 1 (CD-GDD-ALIGN, stage 12):** the row 5 pre-stage has `intensity_pm` 0, so HISS and WIND (driven by flow) are silent; the cue is the **TICK** acoustic class, the hull groan the Graph's LS-22 Hull row names (`LSG:215`), played by Adaptive Audio at the descriptor's anchor on the consequence channel (FT-35) and routed to Adaptive Audio and to the Graph's LS-14 `acoustic` assignment through the single FT-30 amendment (item c). While the hairline is unresolved at k = 1 that TICK must exist for the descriptor; no marker, layer, icon or extra overlay is added, and the no-added-layer rule stands. *Why:* reflexion-log pattern 6 (an invariant asserted at the default must be re-checked when later decisions add reachable states).

**FT-35 Audio independence.** This system plays no audio. The `acoustic` classes (HISS, DRIP, HUM_DROP, CLANK, WIND, TICK, SIZZLE, CREAK) are Adaptive Audio's, played at the same anchor from the same descriptor; a hiss with no visible mist (a dark room) is expected, not a bug; this system never waits for audio, and audio may lead visuals. Both systems take the anchor position from Layout (one source). Adaptive Audio is designed (`adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`): AA-24 plays the eight witness classes at the descriptor's anchor from its true fields, and AC-AA-117 discharges AC-144. *Why:* `IDX:41`.

**FT-36 Accessibility toggles are read, not owned.** The system reads "reduced motion" and "reduce flashing" from Settings (provisional: Settings undesigned) and applies FT-28 and FT-29 only; it owns no setting. *Why:* `PIPE:847`.

**FT-37 Containment (no orphan pixels).** No opaque trail pixel lies outside its own compartment's floor tiles or its host face. Run (body) stamps are authored so their lateral content never exceeds the corridor clearance (a lateral strip of at most 48 px at m and l); head and source stamps may fill a full cell. Clipping to a Layout floor mask and clamping the head stamp to clearance are rejected alternatives (the second would make `trail_size` depend on Layout, breaking FT-6). The Layout clearance read is provisional. **Corridor narrower than a head cell (closing-stage ruling, decided here):** because clipping and clamping are rejected, a corridor whose clearance is below the head cell of any size its anchor can reach (an anchor that can reach sev 4 needs 144 px of clearance at the head) is a **Layout content error** (a lint to Layout and Derelict Authoring, routed in Open Questions), never a runtime fallback; the acceptance fixture uses a corridor at least as wide as the head cell. **Interval reading (confirmed for Layout LY-8 d, 2026-10-27):** the 144 px is a free interval normal to the final run segment at each sample point of the floor run, not a square centred on the run line; the cryo-bay run 24 px from the north wall passes under it. *Why:* an orphan trail on the neighbouring room's floor would break FT-3 (`LSG:970`-`LSG:977`).

**FT-38 MVP slice (what ships first, what waits).** **MVP:** every drawing row of the table of record for the ids the Graph's MVP slice emits (`LSG:228`: `1 LEAK` GAS/COOLANT/FUEL, `2 SHORT`, `3 OVERHEAT`, `4 FREEZE`, `5 BREACH`, `7 OVERLOAD`, `8 SEVERED`, `9 SCRAM`, `20`-`26` as emitted; `6`, `10`, `22`, `23` draw nothing) on the home-ship (5 compartments), derelict A (4) and derelict B (5); the stepped-frame path only (no particle node, FT-21); single-player (no MIRROR path, FT-33); the authored s cells s-frost, s-scorch, s-hairline and s-strain, with s-mist and s-puddle deferred (FT-7); one port shape (the round Foundry frame) for row 26 and the seam rows; variants s 2 / m 1 / l 1 (V/A.10 MVP cut); `TRAIL_EXTENT_SMOOTH` false; the six glow rows of V/A.6; the five drawing hazard groups of the eight MVP faults of `LSG:228`, each with a retained lit capture (AC-75). **Later:** particles for venting after spike S1; MIRROR parity after the routed `SHARED_SUMMARY` fields (VS-B, FT-33); the frozen-puddle presentation hint (VS-A, `LSG:838`); a read-only footprint mark for CHILL/BURN/STRESS (VS-A, FT-31); s-mist and s-puddle once the Graph states the seam-weep severity; further port shapes and the full variant counts; the `DULL_STEPS` 8 scan matrix if the user chooses it (OQ 1). *Why:* the index names this system MVP for MVP item 2 (`game-concept.md`, MVP Definition); a slice that is only implied cannot be scoped into epics.

#### 6. Descriptor-to-visual table of record

*This table is the system's table of record and the input to `/asset-spec system:failure-and-trail-system` (`LSG:979`); Visual/Audio refers to it and does not duplicate it. All seventeen descriptor ids have a row (ids 1-10 and 20-26); mode 1 is three rows by medium; mode 8 inherits mode 1's row for its medium. Behaviour words are the art bible's (`ART:314`). Band: **F** = floor marks (z +1), **W** = Y-sorted wall layer (z +10, host-face origin), **D** = dust and particles (z +30). "TRUTH" rows are consequence-channel and exact under the Filter; the four "draws nothing" rows say who renders that id's channel instead. `intensity_pm` and `since_steps` columns say what each field drives; cells that depend on a Graph field the Graph does not define are PROVISIONAL. Sheets: `trail_shared` stamps (albedo and mask pages, plus normal and specular only on frost and puddle), stepped media strips `vfx_*`.*

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
